# Decisions — one line each, newest first
Owner: the Lead records; the founder decides product choices. Only decisions that change what is built or how we work. Weighty ones get a page in `decisions/`. Superseded lines are struck through, never deleted; when this file passes 100 lines, superseded lines move to `history/`.

| # | Date | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| P-590 | 14 Nov | **Founder, typed before 11:37 IST in Lead 42's session (relayed word for word by Lead 42), on P-589's options: "let's go by C option ... let's keep building this. And let's go full power."** (1) **Option C confirmed:** stay public until the gate before the first real firm's data, then switch to private on his Pro plan with our own runner once B190 has cut minutes (P-589 (3)). Not chosen: A (rented servers, about US$100-300 a month, to go private now) and B (private now with slow checks). (2) **Two cheap steps now:** a "proprietary, all rights reserved" notice and SECURITY.md (repository root) in one small records PR (P-583 step 10; root README stays a later step); the private backup `xpatready-backup` mirrored and refreshed daily once he creates it (P-589 (1)). (3) **"Full power":** keep building at full speed; lanes start at his typed turns (P-348 (5)). (4) Lead 42 told him that after the switch nobody outside can see or copy anything new, but copies already taken stay out there (Unconfirmed: no counsel). (5) **Not yet asked:** an open-core split (public engine on Sample data; rules data and pathways in a private home): the Lead raises it once, as a design question, when the build runs at full power. Impact: no cost now; no time on the milestone. |
| P-589 | 14 Nov | **Founder, typed ~11:1x IST, on A53 (private repository at at most US$20-30 a month; research report in the Lead's private notes, private-repo-options-2026-11-14.md): "I agree with your backup now cut minutes".** (1) **A private backup copy now** (US$0: a private repository he creates on his account, which already has GitHub Pro; the Lead fills it with a full mirror and keeps it current; no Actions in it). (2) **Cut CI minutes first (B190), no check dropped**; measured baseline ~28,700 job-minutes a day (11-13 Nov), target ~4,000. (4) Not chosen: going private now on own machines (capacity ~1/10 of today's load inside US$30, so CI ~10x slower); copy-and-delete (no extra privacy, loses PRs, history, settings, links). Risks named to him: code visible until the gate (14-day views 23 by 8 visitors; copyright stays his, Unconfirmed: no counsel); the postponed GitHub own-runner fee; B190's cut is unproven. |
| P-588 | 14 Nov | **Lead 42's rulings on the case-side contract v0 §9 (desk 02; private notes, desk folder; fresh Opus challenge: ready with changes, 0 Blocker, 14 Major, 9 Minor), 10:0x IST.** (1) Pathway configuration is platform-wide and read-only to firms in the first build (P-585 (3), X-20), **plus a write wall on the platform-wide tables**. (2) The client is a new case-side record and people stay per case, **with changes**: it stores the front office's person or company id; a directly opened case (today's "Open a case") also gets a client, so `clientId` is never left unfilled; the client's kind comes from the program's client type (`Combination.clientType`, SA-3-4), not from the client. (3) **"Active" for PF-15's count is the team's SA-3-47** (from creation until decision or withdrawal) and individual vs corporate is SA-3-4; nothing on main records a decision or withdrawal yet, so a case-side row for it is sized in the 19 Nov count; PF-15's limit is checked inside the case-open transaction (two opens at once cannot both pass). (4) The database fills the firm id for tables under a case, on conditions: a Prisma default on the column (not a required field), consistency checks on the 24 multi-parent tables (foreign-key checks ignore row-level security), triggers run with the caller's rights, and the walls test runs with row-level security on under an app role that owns no tables. (5) The desk's six tables accepted; the two rate-limit tables are the walls test's only named exceptions. (6) Events: add `case.closed`, `case.reopened`, `client.merged` now; hold `filing.ready` until a row consumes it. **Also from the challenge:** `EligibilityPort` must not wrap `engine/runtime/`'s `assess` from the configurator (import cycle): the port and AS-1's scores and grid breakdown are re-placed in a neutral ports folder named in v0.1; 25 direct audit writes in 19 files are counted in the sizing; one owner for a client merge across the person and client records (the case side, called by FO). **Sizing carried into the 19 Nov count: about 15 / 29 / 48 small changes** (Hypothesis until the half-day test). Next: v0.1 with the 14 Majors applied (desk 03), then the Lead places it in `design/`. Impact: no founder time; scope as already counted plus the decision/withdrawal row. |

| P-576 | 14 Nov | **Order of work: critical features first, and within that the order that gives builders the most speed and quality (founder, typed ~09:2x and 09:2x IST 14 Nov: "the sequence should be also in the order which makes the best in class depth, quality, and speed ... critical features coming first"; "think from the dev side also. Applied in Lead B's rows, the case side and the 19 Nov plan. |

(3) **Works before new rows:** the critical path is the C4 lane's port of the C7.6, C9.2, C11.6 and C7.7 packs to release 2 and qa's gate A; Partial capabilities are finished before Not built ones start. (4) **Concept lanes write no new concepts** beyond those approved until their builds catch up. (5) Every lane stops at a labelled PR with a hand-over comment listing what is left (already in every brief); the Lead reads it the same check. (6) The 9 open PRs idle over 24 h stay parked unless on M3's path. Impact (P-254): time, same speed; cost, fewer idle or duplicated sessions; quality, unchanged. |

Seven steps built once and used by every library: search first; a fixed rules check (type, source and reason, no duplicate, references intact, no rule conflict, redundancy, gap or loop); the AI co-pilot drafts a change set that passes the same rules and never writes directly; an impact preview of everything it touches; the person confirms; review by risk (founder: "By risk (Recommended)": wording, labels and aliases direct and logged; new items, meaning changes, rule changes and anything in a released combination need a second person, the expert where P-96 says); a versioned release marked made by person or AI, with roll-back. | P-252 | 2 Nov | **Guarded Add with an AI co-pilot on every reusable library (founder: "Guarded Add + AI co-pilot (Recommended)", 2 Nov, asked by the SA-01B session; a proposal to the product owner on C2, held under P-251; SA-01B v2.1 SA-1B-292).** Founder's words: as the product grows, "human will not have a complete understanding … if they want to introduce a field, why … is there any other field that can be used, repurposed"; so "one co-pilot … used across all the reusable libraries … if somebody wants to add a field, it can say this field already exists … if it doesn't exist, it helps to understand why … and if a new field is required … it should be able to auto-generate it". Scope: one Add on shared fields, sections, answer lists, document types, rules and conditions, forms, programmes and combinations. (1) Search first: names and aliases always; meaning-based matches when AI is on; each match shows why it matched and where it is used. (2) Choose: reuse, add an alias, extend (e.g. a new answer value) or create new. (3) Create new needs a reason and a source: a form box, an official page with a quote, or "set by ExpatReady" plus a reason (with P-240's gates). (4) The co-pilot drafts the whole item: name, type, format, scope, source, where used, and why no existing item fitted. (5) The same second-person review; each item is marked "made by: person / AI" for the counts (impact report D1). (6) Works with AI off: name and alias search plus a manual form with the same mandatory reason and source; AI drafting follows P-175 (live AI off by default, paid runs need a Go). Rows: M2, no new row: steps 1–3, 5 and 6 fold into X3 ("one Add in plain words", shared library; C11.13); M3, new row L-SA11: the AI co-pilot across all libraries plus the guarded Add on the other libraries. (1) The engine, contracts and tests are made lift-ready to ERv2's rules. Engine: resolver, rules and gates, field maps, PDF writer with read-back, answer-key runner, AI drafting and checker. (2) Screens, API and database are a fully tested reference the team rebuilds on ERv2. (3) The hand-over is a package the team pulls; we never push to the team's repositories (AGENTS rule 1); who does the final port is for the founder to agree with the team's tech lead. (4) First a read-only row at US$0 (N-LIFT in Next): run the team's own gates against our engine (content neutrality, hard-coded region, the vendor-SDK import ban, personal-data field classification) and count the violations; the refactor rows are sized from that count, and their milestone (likely M3–M4) is decided after it.
